I’ll bring in a few things and then just stand there for a minute because I feel worn out in a way that doesn’t match what I actually did. It’s frustrating, because I keep telling myself, “Come on, this is not that hard,” but my body feels like it’s already used up before the day really starts.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more critical of yourself lately, or like you’re not good enough, even when you’re doing the same things you’ve always done?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. More than I’d like to admit, honestly. It’s like every little thing at work has started turning into evidence that I’m not doing a good job, even if it’s something tiny.\n\nLike if I walk by a display and notice the sign is a little crooked, I don’t just think, “Oh, I should straighten that.” I’ll think, “How did I miss that? What kind of supervisor doesn’t even notice something right in front of her?” And then I’ll keep thinking about it later, which is ridiculous because it takes two seconds to fix. But in my head it becomes this whole indictment of me.\n\nOr if I’m training someone and they look confused for a minute, I immediately go to, “I’m not explaining this right, I’m failing them, I shouldn’t be in charge of anyone.” I know on some level that people look confused when they’re learning new things, that’s normal. But lately I take it so personally. I replay their face, what I said, whether I sounded impatient, whether I made things worse.\n\nI keep telling myself I’m a useless supervisor, which… saying that out loud sounds harsh. But that is where my mind goes. Even when I’m doing the same job I’ve done for years, I feel like suddenly I’m not good enough to do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently—like eating more or less than usual, or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any clear way. I mean, my eating hasn’t been especially organized, but I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed a real change in appetite or that my clothes are fitting differently or anything like that.\n\nSome days I don’t feel very interested in figuring out meals, but that’s not exactly the same as not being hungry, if that makes sense. I’ll still eat something because it’s lunchtime or because I know I should. And I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I can’t give you a precise number, but nothing has jumped out at me like, “Oh, I’ve lost weight,” or “I’m gaining.”\n\nSo… no, not really. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or maybe feeling restless and unable to sit still, especially when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little of both, actually, which is confusing. It’s not constant, but I have noticed it.\n\nSome days, when I’m just at home and feeling low, I’ll realize I’ve been pacing around the living room for no real reason. Not going anywhere, not doing anything useful, just sort of walking back and forth. And I’ll be picking at my cuticles while I’m doing it, which I know isn’t great, but it’s like my hands need something to do even though the rest of me feels tired.\n\nBut then other days it’s almost the opposite. At work, even walking from the break room back out to the sales floor can feel strangely slow, like I’m moving through syrup or something. It’s not that I physically can’t walk, exactly, but I notice I’m not moving at my usual pace. I’ll think, “Why is this taking me so long?” and then I feel self-conscious about it, like someone might notice I’m dragging.\n\nSo yes, I wouldn’t say it’s dramatic, but I have noticed those kinds of changes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately—like having trouble falling asleep, waking up during the night, or waking up earlier than you’d like and not being able to go back to sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed anything I’d call a clear change. My sleep is… not perfect, but I don’t know that it’s different in a way I can point to.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll lie there and my mind is still going over little things from the day, and that can make it feel like it takes a while to settle, but I don’t know if that’s “trouble falling asleep” in the way you mean. And I can’t say I’m regularly waking up at, you know, 3 or 4 in the morning and unable to get back to sleep or anything like that.\n\nI haven’t been tracking it, so maybe I’m not the best judge. But nothing has stood out to me like, “Oh, my sleep has really changed lately.” So… I’d say no, not really, at least not that I’ve clearly noticed.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way overall? Can you point to when you first started noticing these low moods and loss of interest most days?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about six months, maybe a little more depending on where I start counting from. It wasn’t like I woke up one morning and everything was suddenly gray, you know? It sort of crept in.\n\nAt first I thought I was just tired or in a rut, because I’d still go through the motions. But over time I realized the low, flat feeling was there most days. And the things that usually help me feel like myself weren’t doing that anymore. Like my art classes—I used to really look forward to them, even if I wasn’t making anything amazing, just being there felt good. And my morning walks used to clear my head. But for the last several months, they stopped feeling restorative. I’d go, but it felt more like checking something off a list than actually getting anything from it.\n\nSo if I had to put a time on it, I’d say around six months that this has been going on in a noticeable way.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period of feeling low, did you have any times in the past where you felt significantly depressed for a period of time, and then felt better for at least a few weeks before things got hard again?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can point to a clear earlier episode like that, no. I mean, I’ve had hard stretches in my life, like anyone has, where I felt worn down or unhappy for a while, but I don’t know that I’d call them the same kind of “significantly depressed” period you’re describing.\n\nIt’s hard because when I look back, I can kind of second-guess myself—like, was I actually depressed then, or was I just stressed, or dealing with life being life? I don’t remember a distinct period where I thought, “Oh, this is a depression,” and then it lifted for weeks and I felt clearly back to normal before it came back again.\n\nThis current stretch feels more noticeable to me because it’s lasted and it’s been there most days. Before this, I can’t really give you a definite yes. I’d say… not that I clearly remember.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period of feeling down most days, can you think of a time when you had a similar low mood that lasted for a couple of weeks or more, and then you felt significantly better for a while before this current period started?\n\nPatient: Before this current stretch, I really can’t think of a clear separate time like that. I mean, I’ve had ups and downs, of course, and periods where I was unhappy or worn out, but not something I can confidently say was the same kind of low mood for two weeks or more, and then it went away and I felt significantly better before this started.\n\nThis one feels different mostly because it’s just been so persistent. The low, flat part has been there for about six months now, and it’s been long enough that things I normally rely on—like my art classes or my morning walks—stopped feeling like they were giving me anything back. I still did them sometimes, but they didn’t restore me the way they usually would.\n\nSo I guess my answer is no, not that I can clearly identify. The current period is the one that stands out.\n\nDoctor: Before this current six-month period, can you think of a time when you had a period of feeling down most days, losing interest in things, and having low energy that lasted at least two weeks, and then you felt clearly better for a while before this current period started?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so—not in a way I can clearly point to. I can’t honestly say, “Yes, that was a separate episode, and then it lifted.” This current six-month period is the first one that really stands out to me as something persistent and noticeable day after day.\n\nSo I guess the most accurate answer is: not that I remember clearly.\n\nDoctor: In the past year, have you experienced any major life changes, losses, or stressful events that you feel may have contributed to how you’ve been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think the main thing has been what’s been happening at work with the new district manager. It was more that things started changing, and the tension built up, and I found myself carrying it around with me even when I wasn’t at work. I keep thinking maybe I should be handling it better, because I’ve worked retail supervision for a long time, so I’m used to pressure. But this has felt different somehow.\n\nSo, yes, if I had to point to something in the past year that contributed, it would be that trouble with the new district manager. It’s been about two months since it really started affecting me.\n\nDoctor: Before this current six-month period, have you ever had a time when you felt down most days, lost interest in things, or had low energy that lasted for at least two weeks, even if it eventually went away before things got hard again?\n\nPatient: I’m still having trouble coming up with a clear example before this one. I still can’t really think of an earlier time that fits that pattern clearly — like, a separate two-week stretch that came and then definitely went away.

I’ll bring in a few things and then just stand there for a minute because I feel worn out in a way that doesn’t match what I actually did. But in my head it becomes this whole indictment of me.\n\nOr if I’m training someone and they look confused for a minute, I immediately go to, “I’m not explaining this right, I’m failing them, I shouldn’t be in charge of anyone.” I know on some level that people look confused when they’re learning new things, that’s normal. But lately I take it so personally. I replay their face, what I said, whether I sounded impatient, whether I made things worse.\n\nI keep telling myself I’m a useless supervisor, which… saying that out loud sounds harsh. But that is where my mind goes. Even when I’m doing the same job I’ve done for years, I feel like suddenly I’m not good enough to do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently—like eating more or less than usual, or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any clear way. I mean, my eating hasn’t been especially organized, but I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed a real change in appetite or that my clothes are fitting differently or anything like that.\n\nSome days I don’t feel very interested in figuring out meals, but that’s not exactly the same as not being hungry, if that makes sense. I’ll still eat something because it’s lunchtime or because I know I should. And I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I can’t give you a precise number, but nothing has jumped out at me like, “Oh, I’ve lost weight,” or “I’m gaining.”\n\nSo… no, not really. Not going anywhere, not doing anything useful, just sort of walking back and forth. And I’ll be picking at my cuticles while I’m doing it, which I know isn’t great, but it’s like my hands need something to do even though the rest of me feels tired.\n\nBut then other days it’s almost the opposite. It’s not that I physically can’t walk, exactly, but I notice I’m not moving at my usual pace. That’s the best way I can describe it, like the color has already drained out of the day before I even get dressed for the store.\n\nIt’s not always dramatic crying or anything like that. It’s more like I open my eyes and there’s this weight there, and I think, “Oh… I have to get through another day feeling like this.” I’ll still get up, because I have to, and I can usually make myself do what needs doing, but inside I feel kind of low and flat.\n\nSo… yes, I’d say over the last couple of weeks it’s been there most days, especially in the mornings.\n\nDoctor: Over the last two weeks, have you felt a loss of energy or noticed that even small tasks feel more exhausting than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I have. That’s actually been one of the things that makes me feel a little silly complaining, because it’s not like I’m doing anything terribly strenuous. But even after I’ve been in bed all night, I don’t wake up feeling restored. I’ll think, “Well, I slept, so I should be fine,” and then the smallest things feel like they take so much out of me.\n\nLike opening the store in the morning — things I’ve done for years, unlocking, checking things, getting the day started — it feels heavier than it should. Or one of the staff will ask me a couple of normal questions, nothing difficult, and I can feel myself getting drained just trying to answer and keep my face arranged like I’m fine.\n\nEven groceries… carrying bags in from the car feels like this whole production. I’ll bring in a few things and then just stand there for a minute because I feel worn out in a way that doesn’t match what I actually did. It’s frustrating, because I keep telling myself, “Come on, this is not that hard,” but my body feels like it’s already used up before the day really starts.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling more critical of yourself lately, or like you’re not good enough, even when you’re doing the same things you’ve always done?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. More than I’d like to admit, honestly. It’s like every little thing at work has started turning into evidence that I’m not doing a good job, even if it’s something tiny.\n\nLike if I walk by a display and notice the sign is a little crooked, I don’t just think, “Oh, I should straighten that.” I’ll think, “How did I miss that? What kind of supervisor doesn’t even notice something right in front of her?” And then I’ll keep thinking about it later, which is ridiculous because it takes two seconds to fix. But in my head it becomes this whole indictment of me.\n\nOr if I’m training someone and they look confused for a minute, I immediately go to, “I’m not explaining this right, I’m failing them, I shouldn’t be in charge of anyone.” I know on some level that people look confused when they’re learning new things, that’s normal. But lately I take it so personally. I replay their face, what I said, whether I sounded impatient, whether I made things worse.\n\nI keep telling myself I’m a useless supervisor, which… saying that out loud sounds harsh. But that is where my mind goes. Even when I’m doing the same job I’ve done for years, I feel like suddenly I’m not good enough to do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently—like eating more or less than usual, or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any clear way. I mean, my eating hasn’t been especially organized, but I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed a real change in appetite or that my clothes are fitting differently or anything like that.\n\nSome days I don’t feel very interested in figuring out meals, but that’s not exactly the same as not being hungry, if that makes sense. I’ll still eat something because it’s lunchtime or because I know I should. And I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I can’t give you a precise number, but nothing has jumped out at me like, “Oh, I’ve lost weight,” or “I’m gaining.”\n\nSo… no, not really. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or maybe feeling restless and unable to sit still, especially when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little of both, actually, which is confusing. It’s not constant, but I have noticed it.\n\nSome days, when I’m just at home and feeling low, I’ll realize I’ve been pacing around the living room for no real reason. Not going anywhere, not doing anything useful, just sort of walking back and forth. And I’ll be picking at my cuticles while I’m doing it, which I know isn’t great, but it’s like my hands need something to do even though the rest of me feels tired.\n\nBut then other days it’s almost the opposite. At work, even walking from the break room back out to the sales floor can feel strangely slow, like I’m moving through syrup or something. It’s not that I physically can’t walk, exactly, but I notice I’m not moving at my usual pace. I’ll think, “Why is this taking me so long?” and then I feel self-conscious about it, like someone might notice I’m dragging.\n\nSo yes, I wouldn’t say it’s dramatic, but I have noticed those kinds of changes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately—like having trouble falling asleep, waking up during the night, or waking up earlier than you’d like and not being able to go back to sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed anything I’d call a clear change. My sleep is… not perfect, but I don’t know that it’s different in a way I can point to.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll lie there and my mind is still going over little things from the day, and that can make it feel like it takes a while to settle, but I don’t know if that’s “trouble falling asleep” in the way you mean. And I can’t say I’m regularly waking up at, you know, 3 or 4 in the morning and unable to get back to sleep or anything like that.\n\nI haven’t been tracking it, so maybe I’m not the best judge. But nothing has stood out to me like, “Oh, my sleep has really changed lately.” So… I’d say no, not really, at least not that I’ve clearly noticed.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way overall? Can you point to when you first started noticing these low moods and loss of interest most days?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about six months, maybe a little more depending on where I start counting from. It wasn’t like I woke up one morning and everything was suddenly gray, you know? It sort of crept in.\n\nAt first I thought I was just tired or in a rut, because I’d still go through the motions. But over time I realized the low, flat feeling was there most days. And the things that usually help me feel like myself weren’t doing that anymore. Like my art classes—I used to really look forward to them, even if I wasn’t making anything amazing, just being there felt good. And my morning walks used to clear my head. But for the last several months, they stopped feeling restorative. I’d go, but it felt more like checking something off a list than actually getting anything from it.\n\nSo if I had to put a time on it, I’d say around six months that this has been going on in a noticeable way.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period of feeling low, did you have any times in the past where you felt significantly depressed for a period of time, and then felt better for at least a few weeks before things got hard again?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can point to a clear earlier episode like that, no. I mean, I’ve had hard stretches in my life, like anyone has, where I felt worn down or unhappy for a while, but I don’t know that I’d call them the same kind of “significantly depressed” period you’re describing.\n\nIt’s hard because when I look back, I can kind of second-guess myself—like, was I actually depressed then, or was I just stressed, or dealing with life being life? I don’t remember a distinct period where I thought, “Oh, this is a depression,” and then it lifted for weeks and I felt clearly back to normal before it came back again.\n\nThis current stretch feels more noticeable to me because it’s lasted and it’s been there most days. Before this, I can’t really give you a definite yes. I’d say… not that I clearly remember.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period of feeling down most days, can you think of a time when you had a similar low mood that lasted for a couple of weeks or more, and then you felt significantly better for a while before this current period started?\n\nPatient: Before this current stretch, I really can’t think of a clear separate time like that. I mean, I’ve had ups and downs, of course, and periods where I was unhappy or worn out, but not something I can confidently say was the same kind of low mood for two weeks or more, and then it went away and I felt significantly better before this started.\n\nThis one feels different mostly because it’s just been so persistent. The low, flat part has been there for about six months now, and it’s been long enough that things I normally rely on—like my art classes or my morning walks—stopped feeling like they were giving me anything back. I still did them sometimes, but they didn’t restore me the way they usually would.\n\nSo I guess my answer is no, not that I can clearly identify. The current period is the one that stands out.\n\nDoctor: Before this current six-month period, can you think of a time when you had a period of feeling down most days, losing interest in things, and having low energy that lasted at least two weeks, and then you felt clearly better for a while before this current period started?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so—not in a way I can clearly point to. I know you’re asking very specifically about down most days, losing interest, low energy, lasting at least two weeks, and then having a clear break where I felt better before this current stretch, and… I just don’t have a definite example of that.\n\nI’ve had times where I felt discouraged or tired or kind of emotionally worn out, but when I try to line it up the way you’re describing, it gets blurry. I can’t honestly say, “Yes, that was a separate episode, and then it lifted.” This current six-month period is the first one that really stands out to me as something persistent and noticeable day after day.\n\nSo I guess the most accurate answer is: not that I remember clearly.\n\nDoctor: In the past year, have you experienced any major life changes, losses, or stressful events that you feel may have contributed to how you’ve been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think the main thing has been what’s been happening at work with the new district manager. That really started becoming a problem about two months ago, and honestly, that’s when this whole up-and-down stretch with my mood and energy seemed to begin.\n\nIt wasn’t like one single dramatic event, exactly. It was more that things started changing, and the tension built up, and I found myself carrying it around with me even when I wasn’t at work. I keep thinking maybe I should be handling it better, because I’ve worked retail supervision for a long time, so I’m used to pressure. But this has felt different somehow.\n\nSo, yes, if I had to point to something in the past year that contributed, it would be that trouble with the new district manager. It’s been about two months since it really started affecting me.\n\nDoctor: Before this current six-month period, have you ever had a time when you felt down most days, lost interest in things, or had low energy that lasted for at least two weeks, even if it eventually went away before things got hard again?\n\nPatient: I’m still having trouble coming up with a clear example before this one. I know you’re asking whether there was ever even a smaller period like that — feeling down most days, not interested in things, low energy, for at least a couple of weeks — even if it passed. And I don’t want to say absolutely never, because at my age, of course I’ve had rough patches and stressful times.\n\nBut when I try to remember something that really fits that pattern, I just can’t identify it clearly. No, I still don’t think I can point to an earlier separate time like that — where it lasted at least two weeks, then clearly lifted, and then came back.

What I can say is this current low, flat feeling has been going on for about six months now. It’s been long enough that things I normally rely on to feel like myself — my art classes, my morning walks — they stopped really helping in the way they used to. I’d still maybe go through the motions sometimes, but they didn’t feel restorative, if that makes sense. They didn’t reset me.

So before this? I don’t have a clear “yes, there was another episode and then I got better” answer. This six-month stretch is the one that stands out.

Not that I have a plan, or that I want to hurt myself — I really don’t. It’s more like the thought pops up when I’m feeling worn down, like maybe not having to deal with everything would be a relief. And then I get frightened by the fact that I’m even thinking that way.\n\nI almost didn’t say it out loud because it sounds so dramatic, and I don’t want anyone to overreact, but I also know it’s probably not something I should just keep brushing off.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, work, or spending time with people?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I’m noticing it especially with my art things. I still go through the motions, like I’ll set out my paints on the table or tell myself I should go to pottery class because I know I used to love it. I used to get this little lift just thinking about colors or clay or whatever I was working on.\n\nBut lately, even when I get there, I sort of just… sit there. I’ll have the brushes right in front of me or the clay in my hands, and I’m waiting to feel something familiar, but I just feel kind of blank. It’s more like I open my eyes and there’s this weight there, and I think, “Oh… I have to get through another day feeling like this.” I’ll still get up, because I have to, and I can usually make myself do what needs doing, but inside I feel kind of low and flat.\n\nSo… yes, I’d say over the last couple of weeks it’s been there most days, especially in the mornings.\n\nDoctor: Over the last two weeks, have you felt a loss of energy or noticed that even small tasks feel more exhausting than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I have. That’s actually been one of the things that makes me feel a little silly complaining, because it’s not like I’m doing anything terribly strenuous. But even after I’ve been in bed all night, I don’t wake up feeling restored. More than I’d like to admit, honestly. But that is where my mind goes. Even when I’m doing the same job I’ve done for years, I feel like suddenly I’m not good enough to do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently—like eating more or less than usual, or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any clear way. I mean, my eating hasn’t been especially organized, but I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed a real change in appetite or that my clothes are fitting differently or anything like that.\n\nSome days I don’t feel very interested in figuring out meals, but that’s not exactly the same as not being hungry, if that makes sense. I’ll still eat something because it’s lunchtime or because I know I should. And I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I can’t give you a precise number, but nothing has jumped out at me like, “Oh, I’ve lost weight,” or “I’m gaining.”\n\nSo… no, not really. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or maybe feeling restless and unable to sit still, especially when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little of both, actually, which is confusing. It’s not constant, but I have noticed it.\n\nSome days, when I’m just at home and feeling low, I’ll realize I’ve been pacing around the living room for no real reason. Not going anywhere, not doing anything useful, just sort of walking back and forth. And I’ll be picking at my cuticles while I’m doing it, which I know isn’t great, but it’s like my hands need something to do even though the rest of me feels tired.\n\nBut then other days it’s almost the opposite. At work, even walking from the break room back out to the sales floor can feel strangely slow, like I’m moving through syrup or something. It’s not that I physically can’t walk, exactly, but I notice I’m not moving at my usual pace. I’ll think, “Why is this taking me so long?” and then I feel self-conscious about it, like someone might notice I’m dragging.\n\nSo yes, I wouldn’t say it’s dramatic, but I have noticed those kinds of changes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately—like having trouble falling asleep, waking up during the night, or waking up earlier than you’d like and not being able to go back to sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed anything I’d call a clear change. My sleep is… not perfect, but I don’t know that it’s different in a way I can point to.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll lie there and my mind is still going over little things from the day, and that can make it feel like it takes a while to settle, but I don’t know if that’s “trouble falling asleep” in the way you mean. And I can’t say I’m regularly waking up at, you know, 3 or 4 in the morning and unable to get back to sleep or anything like that.\n\nI haven’t been tracking it, so maybe I’m not the best judge. But nothing has stood out to me like, “Oh, my sleep has really changed lately.” So… I’d say no, not really, at least not that I’ve clearly noticed.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way overall? Can you point to when you first started noticing these low moods and loss of interest most days?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about six months, maybe a little more depending on where I start counting from. It wasn’t like I woke up one morning and everything was suddenly gray, you know? It sort of crept in.\n\nAt first I thought I was just tired or in a rut, because I’d still go through the motions. But over time I realized the low, flat feeling was there most days. And the things that usually help me feel like myself weren’t doing that anymore. Like my art classes—I used to really look forward to them, even if I wasn’t making anything amazing, just being there felt good. And my morning walks used to clear my head. But for the last several months, they stopped feeling restorative. I’d go, but it felt more like checking something off a list than actually getting anything from it.\n\nSo if I had to put a time on it, I’d say around six months that this has been going on in a noticeable way.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period of feeling low, did you have any times in the past where you felt significantly depressed for a period of time, and then felt better for at least a few weeks before things got hard again?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can point to a clear earlier episode like that, no. I mean, I’ve had hard stretches in my life, like anyone has, where I felt worn down or unhappy for a while, but I don’t know that I’d call them the same kind of “significantly depressed” period you’re describing.\n\nIt’s hard because when I look back, I can kind of second-guess myself—like, was I actually depressed then, or was I just stressed, or dealing with life being life? I don’t remember a distinct period where I thought, “Oh, this is a depression,” and then it lifted for weeks and I felt clearly back to normal before it came back again.\n\nThis current stretch feels more noticeable to me because it’s lasted and it’s been there most days. Before this, I can’t really give you a definite yes. I’d say… not that I clearly remember.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period of feeling down most days, can you think of a time when you had a similar low mood that lasted for a couple of weeks or more, and then you felt significantly better for a while before this current period started?\n\nPatient: Before this current stretch, I really can’t think of a clear separate time like that. I mean, I’ve had ups and downs, of course, and periods where I was unhappy or worn out, but not something I can confidently say was the same kind of low mood for two weeks or more, and then it went away and I felt significantly better before this started.\n\nThis one feels different mostly because it’s just been so persistent. The low, flat part has been there for about six months now, and it’s been long enough that things I normally rely on—like my art classes or my morning walks—stopped feeling like they were giving me anything back. I still did them sometimes, but they didn’t restore me the way they usually would.\n\nSo I guess my answer is no, not that I can clearly identify. The current period is the one that stands out.\n\nDoctor: Before this current six-month period, can you think of a time when you had a period of feeling down most days, losing interest in things, and having low energy that lasted at least two weeks, and then you felt clearly better for a while before this current period started?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so—not in a way I can clearly point to. I know you’re asking very specifically about down most days, losing interest, low energy, lasting at least two weeks, and then having a clear break where I felt better before this current stretch, and… I just don’t have a definite example of that.\n\nI’ve had times where I felt discouraged or tired or kind of emotionally worn out, but when I try to line it up the way you’re describing, it gets blurry. I can’t honestly say, “Yes, that was a separate episode, and then it lifted.” This current six-month period is the first one that really stands out to me as something persistent and noticeable day after day.\n\nSo I guess the most accurate answer is: not that I remember clearly.\n\nDoctor: In the past year, have you experienced any major life changes, losses, or stressful events that you feel may have contributed to how you’ve been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think the main thing has been what’s been happening at work with the new district manager. That really started becoming a problem about two months ago, and honestly, that’s when this whole up-and-down stretch with my mood and energy seemed to begin.\n\nIt wasn’t like one single dramatic event, exactly. It was more that things started changing, and the tension built up, and I found myself carrying it around with me even when I wasn’t at work. I keep thinking maybe I should be handling it better, because I’ve worked retail supervision for a long time, so I’m used to pressure. But this has felt different somehow.\n\nSo, yes, if I had to point to something in the past year that contributed, it would be that trouble with the new district manager. It’s been about two months since it really started affecting me.\n\nDoctor: Before this current six-month period, have you ever had a time when you felt down most days, lost interest in things, or had low energy that lasted for at least two weeks, even if it eventually went away before things got hard again?\n\nPatient: I’m still having trouble coming up with a clear example before this one. I know you’re asking whether there was ever even a smaller period like that — feeling down most days, not interested in things, low energy, for at least a couple of weeks — even if it passed. And I don’t want to say absolutely never, because at my age, of course I’ve had rough patches and stressful times.\n\nBut when I try to remember something that really fits that pattern, I just can’t identify it clearly. There were times I was tired, or unhappy, or maybe more withdrawn for a bit, but I don’t remember a distinct stretch where I would say, yes, I was down most days for two weeks or more and lost interest in things, and then it clearly went away. It’s more like normal life ups and downs in my memory, not something I could point to as a separate episode.\n\nSo I guess the honest answer is: not that I can really recall. This current period is the first one that feels clearly persistent enough for me to recognize it that way.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period of feeling down most days, can you think of any time when you had a similar low mood that lasted for at least two weeks and then clearly went away for a while before coming back again?\n\nPatient: I still can’t really think of an earlier time that fits that pattern clearly — like, a separate two-week stretch that came and then definitely went away.\n\nWhat I can say is that lately, in this current period, most mornings I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling. It’s hard to describe without sounding dramatic, but it’s like the color has drained out of things before I even get dressed to go to the store. More than I’d like to admit, honestly. Not going anywhere, not doing anything useful, just sort of walking back and forth. Can you point to when you first started noticing these low moods and loss of interest most days?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about six months, maybe a little more depending on where I start counting from. It wasn’t like I woke up one morning and everything was suddenly gray, you know? It sort of crept in.\n\nAt first I thought I was just tired or in a rut, because I’d still go through the motions. But over time I realized the low, flat feeling was there most days. And the things that usually help me feel like myself weren’t doing that anymore. Like my art classes—I used to really look forward to them, even if I wasn’t making anything amazing, just being there felt good. And my morning walks used to clear my head. But for the last several months, they stopped feeling restorative. I’d go, but it felt more like checking something off a list than actually getting anything from it.\n\nSo if I had to put a time on it, I’d say around six months that this has been going on in a noticeable way.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period of feeling low, did you have any times in the past where you felt significantly depressed for a period of time, and then felt better for at least a few weeks before things got hard again?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can point to a clear earlier episode like that, no. I mean, I’ve had hard stretches in my life, like anyone has, where I felt worn down or unhappy for a while, but I don’t know that I’d call them the same kind of “significantly depressed” period you’re describing.\n\nIt’s hard because when I look back, I can kind of second-guess myself—like, was I actually depressed then, or was I just stressed, or dealing with life being life? I don’t remember a distinct period where I thought, “Oh, this is a depression,” and then it lifted for weeks and I felt clearly back to normal before it came back again.\n\nThis current stretch feels more noticeable to me because it’s lasted and it’s been there most days. Before this, I can’t really give you a definite yes. I’d say… not that I clearly remember.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period of feeling down most days, can you think of a time when you had a similar low mood that lasted for a couple of weeks or more, and then you felt significantly better for a while before this current period started?\n\nPatient: Before this current stretch, I really can’t think of a clear separate time like that. I mean, I’ve had ups and downs, of course, and periods where I was unhappy or worn out, but not something I can confidently say was the same kind of low mood for two weeks or more, and then it went away and I felt significantly better before this started.\n\nThis one feels different mostly because it’s just been so persistent. The low, flat part has been there for about six months now, and it’s been long enough that things I normally rely on—like my art classes or my morning walks—stopped feeling like they were giving me anything back. I still did them sometimes, but they didn’t restore me the way they usually would.\n\nSo I guess my answer is no, not that I can clearly identify. The current period is the one that stands out.\n\nDoctor: Before this current six-month period, can you think of a time when you had a period of feeling down most days, losing interest in things, and having low energy that lasted at least two weeks, and then you felt clearly better for a while before this current period started?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so—not in a way I can clearly point to. I know you’re asking very specifically about down most days, losing interest, low energy, lasting at least two weeks, and then having a clear break where I felt better before this current stretch, and… I just don’t have a definite example of that.\n\nI’ve had times where I felt discouraged or tired or kind of emotionally worn out, but when I try to line it up the way you’re describing, it gets blurry. I can’t honestly say, “Yes, that was a separate episode, and then it lifted.” This current six-month period is the first one that really stands out to me as something persistent and noticeable day after day.\n\nSo I guess the most accurate answer is: not that I remember clearly.\n\nDoctor: In the past year, have you experienced any major life changes, losses, or stressful events that you feel may have contributed to how you’ve been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think the main thing has been what’s been happening at work with the new district manager. It was more that things started changing, and the tension built up, and I found myself carrying it around with me even when I wasn’t at work. I keep thinking maybe I should be handling it better, because I’ve worked retail supervision for a long time, so I’m used to pressure. It’s been about two months since it really started affecting me.\n\nDoctor: Before this current six-month period, have you ever had a time when you felt down most days, lost interest in things, or had low energy that lasted for at least two weeks, even if it eventually went away before things got hard again?\n\nPatient: I’m still having trouble coming up with a clear example before this one. I know you’re asking whether there was ever even a smaller period like that — feeling down most days, not interested in things, low energy, for at least a couple of weeks — even if it passed. And I don’t want to say absolutely never, because at my age, of course I’ve had rough patches and stressful times.\n\nBut when I try to remember something that really fits that pattern, I just can’t identify it clearly. There were times I was tired, or unhappy, or maybe more withdrawn for a bit, but I don’t remember a distinct stretch where I would say, yes, I was down most days for two weeks or more and lost interest in things, and then it clearly went away. It’s hard to describe without sounding dramatic, but it’s like the color has drained out of things before I even get dressed to go to the store. I’ll be lying there or getting ready, and there’s just this weight to the morning that wasn’t always there.\n\nBut before this current stretch? I’m not coming up with a clear earlier episode where it was like that for a couple weeks and then lifted.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period of feeling down most days, can you think of any time when you had a low mood that lasted for at least two weeks, and then you felt clearly better for a while before this current period started?\n\nPatient: No, I still don’t think I can point to an earlier separate time like that — where it lasted at least two weeks, then clearly lifted, and then came back.\n\nWhat I can say is this current low, flat feeling has been going on for about six months now.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I still can’t really identify an earlier separate period like that, no. The part I can describe clearly is this current stretch — the low, flat feeling has been going on for about six months now.

And it’s not just a few bad days here and there. It’s been long enough that the things I usually count on to bring me back to myself, like going to art classes or taking my morning walks, stopped feeling restorative. I might still do them, but they don’t have that same effect of clearing my head or making me feel reset.

So when you ask about something before this — several weeks of major low mood, then being completely normal for a couple months — I honestly don’t have a clear example of that. This six-month period is the one I can really point to.

What kind of supervisor doesn’t even notice something right in front of her?” And then I’ll keep thinking about it later, which is ridiculous because it takes two seconds to fix. But in my head it becomes this whole indictment of me.\n\nOr if I’m training someone and they look confused for a minute, I immediately go to, “I’m not explaining this right, I’m failing them, I shouldn’t be in charge of anyone.” I know on some level that people look confused when they’re learning new things, that’s normal. But lately I take it so personally. I replay their face, what I said, whether I sounded impatient, whether I made things worse.\n\nI keep telling myself I’m a useless supervisor, which… saying that out loud sounds harsh. But that is where my mind goes. Even when I’m doing the same job I’ve done for years, I feel like suddenly I’m not good enough to do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently—like eating more or less than usual, or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any clear way. I mean, my eating hasn’t been especially organized, but I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed a real change in appetite or that my clothes are fitting differently or anything like that.\n\nSome days I don’t feel very interested in figuring out meals, but that’s not exactly the same as not being hungry, if that makes sense. I’ll still eat something because it’s lunchtime or because I know I should. And I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I can’t give you a precise number, but nothing has jumped out at me like, “Oh, I’ve lost weight,” or “I’m gaining.”\n\nSo… no, not really. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or maybe feeling restless and unable to sit still, especially when you're feeling down?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little of both, actually, which is confusing. It’s not constant, but I have noticed it.\n\nSome days, when I’m just at home and feeling low, I’ll realize I’ve been pacing around the living room for no real reason. Not going anywhere, not doing anything useful, just sort of walking back and forth. And I’ll be picking at my cuticles while I’m doing it, which I know isn’t great, but it’s like my hands need something to do even though the rest of me feels tired.\n\nBut then other days it’s almost the opposite. At work, even walking from the break room back out to the sales floor can feel strangely slow, like I’m moving through syrup or something. It’s not that I physically can’t walk, exactly, but I notice I’m not moving at my usual pace. I’ll think, “Why is this taking me so long?” and then I feel self-conscious about it, like someone might notice I’m dragging.\n\nSo yes, I wouldn’t say it’s dramatic, but I have noticed those kinds of changes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately—like having trouble falling asleep, waking up during the night, or waking up earlier than you’d like and not being able to go back to sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed anything I’d call a clear change. My sleep is… not perfect, but I don’t know that it’s different in a way I can point to.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll lie there and my mind is still going over little things from the day, and that can make it feel like it takes a while to settle, but I don’t know if that’s “trouble falling asleep” in the way you mean. And I can’t say I’m regularly waking up at, you know, 3 or 4 in the morning and unable to get back to sleep or anything like that.\n\nI haven’t been tracking it, so maybe I’m not the best judge. But nothing has stood out to me like, “Oh, my sleep has really changed lately.” So… I’d say no, not really, at least not that I’ve clearly noticed.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way overall? Can you point to when you first started noticing these low moods and loss of interest most days?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about six months, maybe a little more depending on where I start counting from. It wasn’t like I woke up one morning and everything was suddenly gray, you know? It sort of crept in.\n\nAt first I thought I was just tired or in a rut, because I’d still go through the motions. But over time I realized the low, flat feeling was there most days. And the things that usually help me feel like myself weren’t doing that anymore. Like my art classes—I used to really look forward to them, even if I wasn’t making anything amazing, just being there felt good. And my morning walks used to clear my head. But for the last several months, they stopped feeling restorative. I’d go, but it felt more like checking something off a list than actually getting anything from it.\n\nSo if I had to put a time on it, I’d say around six months that this has been going on in a noticeable way.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period of feeling low, did you have any times in the past where you felt significantly depressed for a period of time, and then felt better for at least a few weeks before things got hard again?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can point to a clear earlier episode like that, no. I mean, I’ve had hard stretches in my life, like anyone has, where I felt worn down or unhappy for a while, but I don’t know that I’d call them the same kind of “significantly depressed” period you’re describing.\n\nIt’s hard because when I look back, I can kind of second-guess myself—like, was I actually depressed then, or was I just stressed, or dealing with life being life? I don’t remember a distinct period where I thought, “Oh, this is a depression,” and then it lifted for weeks and I felt clearly back to normal before it came back again.\n\nThis current stretch feels more noticeable to me because it’s lasted and it’s been there most days. Before this, I can’t really give you a definite yes. I’d say… not that I clearly remember.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period of feeling down most days, can you think of a time when you had a similar low mood that lasted for a couple of weeks or more, and then you felt significantly better for a while before this current period started?\n\nPatient: Before this current stretch, I really can’t think of a clear separate time like that. I mean, I’ve had ups and downs, of course, and periods where I was unhappy or worn out, but not something I can confidently say was the same kind of low mood for two weeks or more, and then it went away and I felt significantly better before this started.\n\nThis one feels different mostly because it’s just been so persistent. The low, flat part has been there for about six months now, and it’s been long enough that things I normally rely on—like my art classes or my morning walks—stopped feeling like they were giving me anything back. I still did them sometimes, but they didn’t restore me the way they usually would.\n\nSo I guess my answer is no, not that I can clearly identify. The current period is the one that stands out.\n\nDoctor: Before this current six-month period, can you think of a time when you had a period of feeling down most days, losing interest in things, and having low energy that lasted at least two weeks, and then you felt clearly better for a while before this current period started?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so—not in a way I can clearly point to. I know you’re asking very specifically about down most days, losing interest, low energy, lasting at least two weeks, and then having a clear break where I felt better before this current stretch, and… I just don’t have a definite example of that.\n\nI’ve had times where I felt discouraged or tired or kind of emotionally worn out, but when I try to line it up the way you’re describing, it gets blurry. I can’t honestly say, “Yes, that was a separate episode, and then it lifted.” This current six-month period is the first one that really stands out to me as something persistent and noticeable day after day.\n\nSo I guess the most accurate answer is: not that I remember clearly.\n\nDoctor: In the past year, have you experienced any major life changes, losses, or stressful events that you feel may have contributed to how you’ve been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think the main thing has been what’s been happening at work with the new district manager. I keep thinking maybe I should be handling it better, because I’ve worked retail supervision for a long time, so I’m used to pressure. But this has felt different somehow.\n\nSo, yes, if I had to point to something in the past year that contributed, it would be that trouble with the new district manager. It’s been about two months since it really started affecting me.\n\nDoctor: Before this current six-month period, have you ever had a time when you felt down most days, lost interest in things, or had low energy that lasted for at least two weeks, even if it eventually went away before things got hard again?\n\nPatient: I’m still having trouble coming up with a clear example before this one. I know I’ve kind of answered this already, but no, I still can’t think of a separate earlier spell like that before this current one. The thing I can really put my finger on is that the low, flat feeling has been going on for about six months now.

It’s been long enough that the things that usually help me feel like myself again just haven’t been doing that. Like my art classes — normally those give me a little lift, or at least make me feel connected to something — and my morning walks usually clear my head. But during this stretch, they stopped feeling restorative in that way. I might still go through the motions, but they don’t really bring me back.

So if you’re asking whether there was an earlier several-week low period and then a couple months of feeling completely normal before this, I don’t have a clear example of that. This six-month period is the one that stands out.

I’ll think, “Why is this taking me so long?” and then I feel self-conscious about it, like someone might notice I’m dragging.\n\nSo yes, I wouldn’t say it’s dramatic, but I have noticed those kinds of changes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately—like having trouble falling asleep, waking up during the night, or waking up earlier than you’d like and not being able to go back to sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed anything I’d call a clear change. My sleep is… not perfect, but I don’t know that it’s different in a way I can point to.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’ll lie there and my mind is still going over little things from the day, and that can make it feel like it takes a while to settle, but I don’t know if that’s “trouble falling asleep” in the way you mean. And I can’t say I’m regularly waking up at, you know, 3 or 4 in the morning and unable to get back to sleep or anything like that.\n\nI haven’t been tracking it, so maybe I’m not the best judge. But nothing has stood out to me like, “Oh, my sleep has really changed lately.” So… I’d say no, not really, at least not that I’ve clearly noticed.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way overall? Can you point to when you first started noticing these low moods and loss of interest most days?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about six months, maybe a little more depending on where I start counting from. It wasn’t like I woke up one morning and everything was suddenly gray, you know? It sort of crept in.\n\nAt first I thought I was just tired or in a rut, because I’d still go through the motions. But over time I realized the low, flat feeling was there most days. And the things that usually help me feel like myself weren’t doing that anymore. Like my art classes—I used to really look forward to them, even if I wasn’t making anything amazing, just being there felt good. And my morning walks used to clear my head. But for the last several months, they stopped feeling restorative. I’d go, but it felt more like checking something off a list than actually getting anything from it.\n\nSo if I had to put a time on it, I’d say around six months that this has been going on in a noticeable way.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period of feeling low, did you have any times in the past where you felt significantly depressed for a period of time, and then felt better for at least a few weeks before things got hard again?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can point to a clear earlier episode like that, no. I mean, I’ve had hard stretches in my life, like anyone has, where I felt worn down or unhappy for a while, but I don’t know that I’d call them the same kind of “significantly depressed” period you’re describing.\n\nIt’s hard because when I look back, I can kind of second-guess myself—like, was I actually depressed then, or was I just stressed, or dealing with life being life? I don’t remember a distinct period where I thought, “Oh, this is a depression,” and then it lifted for weeks and I felt clearly back to normal before it came back again.\n\nThis current stretch feels more noticeable to me because it’s lasted and it’s been there most days. Before this, I can’t really give you a definite yes. I’d say… not that I clearly remember.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period of feeling down most days, can you think of a time when you had a similar low mood that lasted for a couple of weeks or more, and then you felt significantly better for a while before this current period started?\n\nPatient: Before this current stretch, I really can’t think of a clear separate time like that. I mean, I’ve had ups and downs, of course, and periods where I was unhappy or worn out, but not something I can confidently say was the same kind of low mood for two weeks or more, and then it went away and I felt significantly better before this started.\n\nThis one feels different mostly because it’s just been so persistent. The low, flat part has been there for about six months now, and it’s been long enough that things I normally rely on—like my art classes or my morning walks—stopped feeling like they were giving me anything back.
